| S-DARK-33.2 | **Held-out acceptance and ROC of the induced automata.** ALERGIA (alpha 0.05), MDL-merge, Markov-1/2 and a 20-state HMM fitted on 80% of MD+H (seals; all types with the object type as start symbol); threshold = 5th percentile of the per-sign log-likelihood on the other 20%. Genuine: held-out sites (297 seals / 451 texts), the 324 IM77-only certainly-new texts (S-DARK-27; 164 seals; M->W via the completed bridge, 0 unbridged tokens). Negatives from the same texts: within-text shuffle, within-slot shuffle, random unigram strings. Controls with the same pipeline (cycle 5): a strict designed code (6 ordered optional fields), the loop 25 planted frame, Ur III legends, Linear B lines, Proto-Elamite lines, a Markov-2 clone of the Indus seals, shuffled Indus. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop33.py 2`, `5`) | **Genuine held-out sites accepted 0.94-0.97 (seals), IM77-new seals 0.77-0.82; but within-text shuffles of the same texts are accepted 0.62-0.86 and random strings 0.58-0.92. AUC genuine vs shuffle: held-out sites 0.72-0.77 (texts >= 3 signs 0.82-0.85), IM77-new 0.63-0.72; vs slot shuffle 0.53-0.62.** Controls: strict code AUC 0.97-0.99 (rejects 93-96% of shuffles), planted frame 0.91-0.94, Ur III legends 0.90-0.95, Markov-2 clone of Indus 0.82-0.85, Proto-Elamite 0.79-0.82, Linear B 0.71-0.76, shuffled Indus 0.49-0.50. All three levels within 0.03. | **A compact automaton does not behave like a designed code on new texts.** It accepts genuine new material, but it cannot tell a genuine Indus text from its own shuffle much better than chance beyond the frame (AUC 0.72-0.83 vs 0.97-0.99 for a real code and 0.90-0.95 for Ur III legends); the Indus texts separate no better than a Markov-2 clone of themselves. What the automaton learns is the frame (first/last slots); the inside is unpredictable to it. The 'business code' premise fails this test on all levels. |
| S-DARK-33.3 | **Reading the automaton as a form.** ALERGIA (alpha 0.05, root never merged) on all MD+H seals (CLASS alphabet, 52 symbols) and on all 2,946 texts with the object type as start symbol; every state named by its dominant incoming and outgoing symbols and mean position; loops (edges to an earlier position), second paths, and forbidden class bigrams (expected >= 8 under independence, 0 observed in fit) listed; forbidden bigrams and the class order tested as predictions on held-out sites and the 324 IM77-new texts against within-text shuffles (200x). seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop33.py 3`; `loop33_automaton_*.json/.dot`) | **Seals: 14-15 states; all types: 61-64 states.** The states are the frame: START -> after-OPENER (CONN 319/400) -> MIDDLE HUB (fish-mark self-loop, numeral phrases, lexicon run, leaf) -> PRE-CLOSER (free titles) or PRE-CLOSER-2 (fixed terms w142/w575/w585/TITLE -> jar) -> END HUB (end 0.79, suffix loop). Extras the hand grammar lacks: loops END HUB -> numeral phrase (27x), -> OPEN (13x), -> CONN (11x) and LEXICON -> OPEN (60x) = second units; a separate state for the fixed pre-jar terms; 18 forbidden bigrams (jar->jar, jar->TITLE, OPEN->fish mark, CONN->tree, jar<->arrow, ...). **Prediction held: forbidden bigrams on held-out sites 5 of 969 (shuffle median 39 [30-48]), IM77-new 4 of 534 (20 [12-26]); all-types 6/1,305 and 9/967 vs 34-39 and 32-33. Class order Kendall tau fit vs held-out 0.51-0.59.** Same on all levels. | **The automaton is the frame plus an unordered hub.** Its only obligatory element is the end; the middle is a hub that returns to itself, so any phrase type may follow any other (S-DARK-26 again), with the lexicon signs all at relative position 0.3-0.5 and no order among them. The new facts are the second-unit loops and the forbidden transitions, which generalise to new sites and new texts (1/7 of the shuffle rate). The 'form' is: [opener connective]? phrases* [titles | fixed term] closer suffix* [second unit]? - a formulaic legend, not a fixed-field record. |
| S-DARK-33.4 | **Size of the code.** Seal automata (ALERGIA 14-15 states; Markov-1/2 for comparison) on all MD+H seals, RAW and CLASS alphabets; best-first enumeration of all strings with P >= 1e-3 / 1e-4 / 1e-5; share seen in the fit set; probability mass of the enumerated set; model P(next text already seen) vs Good-Turing singleton share. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop33.py 4`) | **At P >= 1e-4 the automaton generates ~600 (RAW) to ~770 (CLASS) texts, 18-28% of them seen; the set holds only 27-42% of the probability mass (58-73% is in strings rarer than 1e-4).** P(next seal text new): automaton 0.90 (RAW) / 0.73-0.77 (CLASS), Good-Turing 0.82 / 0.70 on the same 1,259 seals (S309: 0.95 for 3+-sign texts, whole corpus). Markov-2 overfits (P(new) 0.67, 47% of its 1e-4 strings seen). Same on all levels. | **The generator is open-ended, not a listable code.** Most of the automaton's mass is in a long tail of individually rare strings, so the low seen/possible ratio is a property of the grammar (a hub of freely combining phrases) rather than of under-sampling a fixed message set; it is consistent with S309's 95% new-text rate without needing the unseen signs. A compact automaton describes the frame and the phrase types, but the number of distinct texts it licenses is unbounded for practical purposes. |
| S-DARK-34.1 | **Checksum hunt, sign targets.** For each hidden position (first, second, penultimate, last, the closer with its position hidden, closer-vs-name-final) train a decision tree / random forest / 5-NN on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa seals (1,224) from everything else on the object (counts of the other signs, neighbours of the hidden slot, length, sub-type, emblem/material/boss/shape, and sum-of-sign-indices mod 2..13 so a modular check is learnable); score on seals of the other sites (291) and the 319 IM77-only texts of S-DARK-27.3 (M -> W through the completed bridge). Baselines: modal class and slot-grammar modal (frame class of the rest). Planted controls through the same pipeline: target := class[sum of other indices mod 8] (checksum), target := lookup[first other sign] (class sign), labels shuffled. Three merge levels (`tools/dark_loop34.py`, `dark_loop34_c1.py`) | Planted checksum recovered 1.00 / 1.00 (sites / IM77) for every target; planted class sign 0.86-0.96; shuffled labels fall to the modal rate (0.3-0.7). Real targets, best model vs slot baseline, sites then IM77: first sign 0.68-0.71 vs 0.60 (IM77 0.70 vs 0.68, P = 0.2); second sign 0.71-0.73 vs 0.63 (0.74 vs 0.70); penultimate 0.73 vs 0.69 (0.68 vs 0.63); last sign 0.51-0.56 vs 0.40 (0.38-0.42 vs 0.25); which closer 0.69 vs 0.63 (0.60 vs 0.52); closer-vs-name 0.68-0.73 vs 0.65 (0.65 vs 0.60). Carrying features are all frame rules: next sign = W2 -> opener; opener -> W2; tall-3 or fish before the end -> arrow W520; W550 -> W527; jar elsewhere -> not jar-final. The sum-mod-k features never rank above noise (importance 0.02-0.03, same as in the shuffled runs). Levels agree within 0.05 | **No check sign and no class sign among the sign positions.** Every position is predictable from the rest only to the extent that GRAMMAR.md already says (opener-connective pairing, the ten closer units), i.e. 4-14 points above the slot baseline against a planted ceiling of 100. The last sign is the most 'determined' slot (0.51-0.56) and that determination is lexical (which closer unit), not arithmetic (data/derived/dark/loop34_cycle1.txt) |
| S-DARK-34.2 | **Checksum hunt, object-fact targets and rule extraction.** Same pipeline as 34.1 with the emblem class (11 classes, Wells codes checked against IM77 field-symbol descriptions on 2,743 aligned pairs), material, boss class and shape class as targets, predicted from the whole text (+ the other facts); planted checksum, planted class sign and shuffled labels as controls; then a depth-3 tree per target with every leaf of train precision >= 0.85 tested on the hold-outs with a < 3/n bound (`tools/dark_loop34_c2.py`) | Held-out sites: emblem 0.71-0.75 vs modal 0.71 (IM77-only seals 0.44 = modal; P 0.05-0.4), material 0.92 = modal, boss 0.67 vs 0.60 and shape 0.88-0.90 vs 0.79, but boss and shape are carried by the sub-type code (SEAL:R = round seal -> circular, no boss), the text features never rank; planted checksum 1.00, planted class sign 0.89-0.94, shuffled = modal. Rules recovered: 'tall-3 (W33) before the end -> arrow W520' (train 23-24/24; sites 2/2 and 3/4, IM77 4/4 and 2/2), 'plain fish before the closer -> W520' (15/16; 2/2, 3/3), 'W550 -> W527' (13/16), 'no jar elsewhere + fish -> closer-final' 0.89 train but 0.80-0.87 held-out; emblem: no leaf predicts anything but unicorn. Three levels agree | **No emblem, material, boss or shape is a function of the text, and no sign is a function of them** (S15/S28/S365 re-found with a learner validated at 1.00 on a planted rule). The only exceptionless rules recovered are the known closer units of S289/S299, each with 2-4 held-out cases (bounds < 3/2 to < 3/4), so they are confirmed as tendencies, not proven as rules (data/derived/dark/loop34_cycle2.txt) |
| S-DARK-34.3 | **Checksum hunt, numeric: is the stroke numeral a function of the rest of the text?** Texts with one valued numeral (S204 values; W1/W2/W31 excluded as markers): train MD + Harappa 576 (344 seals, 214 tablets), test other sites 93 and IM77-only 66. (a) Same tree / forest / 5-NN pipeline with the numeral hidden, planted checksum and class sign, shuffled labels; (b) exact-match rate and Spearman rho of the value against n signs, n+1, n distinct, n fish, n non-frame signs, position, sum of W indices mod 10 / mod 7, rank-sum mod 9, each against a within-(site, type) permutation null (2,000 reps); (c) annealed additive sign-to-value map (0-9 on the 60 commonest signs, 12 restarts) fitted on train, scored held-out, against three permuted-value anneals and a planted additive target. Three levels (`tools/dark_loop34_c3.py`) | (a) Sites 0.46-0.51 vs modal 0.41 / slot 0.37; IM77 0.58-0.64 vs 0.45 / 0.33; planted checksum 1.00, class sign 0.85-0.91, shuffled 0.31-0.40 / 0.47-0.52. The only features above noise are prev_W705 / count_W705 (the 'U-stroke + tall 3' fixed term, S299); seals only 0.44 vs 0.42 (P = 0.45). (b) No function's exact-match rate beats its null (n signs 0.18 vs 0.20; mod 10 0.08 vs 0.10; mod 7 0.14 vs 0.12, P = 0.06). Value vs length rho -0.10 train, -0.06 sites (P = 0.57), -0.35 IM77 (P < 0.001), -0.11 pooled (P = 0.002): longer texts carry smaller numerals (frozen tall-2 terms in long seal texts, 3-8 counts in short texts). (c) Real anneal held-out -0.02 / +0.04 / -0.01 (sites), -0.17 to -0.25 (IM77), inside the permuted-value spread (-0.03 to -0.22; -0.17 to -0.41); planted additive truth 0.79 -> recovered 0.72 | **The numeral is not a checksum of the text.** Its only predictable component is lexical (W705 takes tall-3) and its only correlate is text length, with the wrong sign for a count-of-signs code and chance-level exact matches. Closes the numeric arm alongside S-DARK-5.1-5.4b (data/derived/dark/loop34_cycle3.txt) |
| S-DARK-34.4 | **Checksum hunt, exceptionless-rule census.** Every one-antecedent rule (sign present; first / last / before-closer sign; closer unit; sub-type; length; emblem / material / boss / shape; numeral present / value; sum of indices mod 3/5/7/10 -> one of nine targets) that holds with precision 1.0 on >= 10 MD + Harappa seals, tested on other-site seals (291) and the 319 IM77-only texts; survivor = 0 exceptions on >= 3 held-out cases; controls: consequents permuted within sub-type (3 reps) and a planted emblem := lookup[closer]. Planted emblem := f(closer): 22 survivors with supports 100-500 | **No exceptionless rule links any element of a seal to any other beyond the known closer units, and the one survivor is a 16-case tendency.** With a planted class sign the same census returns 22 rules; on the real seals it returns the base rate. The seals carry no redundancy beyond the frame (data/derived/dark/loop34_cycle4.txt) |
| S-DARK-32.3 | **Typology classifier, cycle 3: granularity-matched library (9 corpora with 110-686 types / 2,000 tokens, 3 per class) and an Indus-vs-surrogate audit** (`tools/dark_loop32_c2.py --gran 0.4 2.5`, `tools/dark_loop32_c3audit.py`; loop32_c3_*.txt). Same nested LOCO, permutation null (30), binary and 3-class; features that separate the real Indus from Markov-2 / bigram / slot-shuffled Indus; features where Indus is outside every language corpus's range; full-space nearest neighbours | Granularity-matched: 3-class **0.22 without numeral features (P = 0.55), 0.67 with them (P = 0.065; 3-NN 0.56, P = 0.03)**, the gain entirely from numeral share / runs (A 31-68 % numerals vs L, D 0-10 %; Indus 12 %, runs 7 %, numerals leaning to the START -0.29); binary 0.44 < 0.67 baseline. Indus -> L 0.88-0.94 under the numeral model but Markov-2 0.90 and bigram 0.88 too; slot-shuffled -> A. Audit: Indus differs from Markov-2 on closer entropy 0.72 vs 0.96, top-10 closer share 0.64 vs 0.42, fixed pairs 0.71 vs 0.45, slot-vs-bigram +0.21 vs +0.78 bits, uniq_vs_bigram 0.83 vs 0.94 (|z| > 19), all INSIDE the language range. Outside the language range: det_loy50_max 0.35 vs 0.46-0.84, slot_exclusivity 0.07 vs 0.10-0.35, n80_first 1.11 vs 0.26-1.01, and the two numeral features. Full space: seq_raw nearest IM77 2.0, Markov-2 Indus 3.8, proto-cuneiform 4.3, Linear B syllables 4.8, Ur III names 5.1, Proto-Elamite 5.2 (raw / strong / all identical order; IM77 nearest Markov-2 3.0) | **Indus is closer to a Markov-2 chain of itself than to any known short-text system; the typology is not decidable from this battery** (only numeral annotation separates accounting, and Indus sits between accounting and language on it). The one non-numeral metric that puts Indus outside every language corpus is the absence of a loyal class sign (det_loy50_max, S-DARK-25); the metrics that make Indus 'formulaic' (closer paradigm, fixed order, low middle uniqueness) are shared with Ur III and Linear B lines. P(designed character code) <= 0.07 in every run; slot-shuffle control behaves as required, Markov-2 control does not. Nothing read, nothing 'cracked'. |
| S366 | **Turing test for the credential grammar** (`tools/strat_adequacy.py`; `data/derived/strat_adequacy.txt`/`.json`; counts from the older `merged-corpus-canonical.json` build, S-DARK-23). Best generative model of a text, fitted on the 4,411 Mohenjo-daro + Harappa texts with the S310 parse: P(opener) × P(marker | opener) × P(middle: order-1 chain with continuation-count backoff) × P(closer | opener present) × P(qualifier string | closer) × P(suffix | closer). 100 synthetic corpora of the same size and object mix; battery of 50 statistics the model is not fitted on (length distribution, unique-text share, nesting, cross-site sharing of 3-runs and texts, MI at distance 1–4, repeated-sign and adjacent-double rates, fish order, long-range attraction and avoidance pairs, middle-initial vs final sets, numeral attachments, positional entropies from both ends, Zipf slope per slot, same middle under different closer, sign/bigram/trigram hapax shares, seal vs tablet length, opener–suffix co-occurrence); real value, synthetic mean ± sd, z, outside-range flag. Mechanisms added one at a time and greedily: object-type effect, site effect, middle lexicon with CRP reuse, closer given the middle's last sign, frame package (opener+marker+closer+suffix jointly), order-2 middle chain, open sign inventory (Good-Turing new-sign mass, S309), whole-text reuse (one CRP per site, θ calibrated to the distinct-text share; a single global CRP was tried first and failed: it spread stock texts over both cities, text3_multisite 0.22 vs 0.02, z = −20). Held-out: 930 texts from 73 other sites with the model fitted on MD+H. Controls: a no-slot order-2 chain as generator; the slot grammar fitted to a no-slot Markov corpus; the order-2 chain refitted to its own output (false-alarm floor of the battery); a planted Tamil syllabic corpus (synth.py) | **Baseline M0: 36 of 50 statistics outside the synthetic range, 35 with |z| > 3, Σ|z| = 378.** Missing structure by |z|: repeated whole texts (texts with ≥ 3 copies 107 vs 19, z = 28; 4-sign runs with ≥ 3 copies 171 vs 33, z = 27), **long-range attraction pairs 127 vs 19 (z = 27)**, unique-text share 0.58 vs 0.76 (z = −27), seal − tablet length 1.36 vs 0.0 (z = 19), **long-range avoidance pairs 272 vs 102 (z = 18)**, bigram conditional entropy 2.77 vs 3.15 (z = −18), **same middle under a different closer 0.20 vs 0.79 (z = −18)**, MI at distance 2 2.49 vs 1.97 (z = 18), trigram hapax share (z = −16), hapax signs 0.35 vs 0.19 (z = 12), **repeated-sign rate 0.043 vs 0.099 (z = −10)**. Single mechanisms: site-local text reuse Σ|z| 222 (fixes unique share, same-middle-different-closer, attraction pairs, MI d4); middle lexicon 271 (fixes MI d1–d4 and attraction pairs, but leaves same-middle-different-closer at z = −35: middles are not recombined freely with closers); closer-given-last-middle-sign 305 (fixes mean length, middle length vs closer, end entropies); order-2 middle 302 (start entropies); object type 347 (tablet suffix, jar-final share, opener–suffix exclusion); site 376, package 380, open inventory 370 do nothing alone. Greedy path: textreuse (221) → closerdep (181) → type (151) → open (137) → site (133). **M* = textreuse + closerdep + type + open + site: 23 of 50 outside, 16 with |z| > 3, Σ|z| = 134, which is at the battery's false-alarm floor (true generator refitted to its own sample: 19 of 50 outside, Σ|z| = 141).** Still outside under M*: long-range avoidance 272 vs 170 (z = 8) and attraction 127 vs 61 (z = 6), repeated-sign rate 0.043 vs 0.092 (z = −8), bigram conditional entropy (z = −6), length sd 2.02 vs 2.33 (z = −6), texts with two numerals 0.044 vs 0.062 (z = −4), start-position entropies (z = −3 to −5), hapax shares (z = 3–5), fish order 0.86 vs 0.75 (z = 2.4). Held-out sites: M0 23/50, Σ|z| 148; M* 19/50, Σ|z| 122 (at the floor); the biggest held-out gap is again long-range attraction (26 vs 4 pairs, z = 12). Controls: no-slot order-2 chain 25/50, Σ|z| 247 (**worse than M* but better than the baseline slot grammar**); slot grammar fitted to a no-slot Markov corpus 34/50, Σ|z| 401 (misfit detected); planted Tamil, order-1 chain 20/46, Σ|z| 180 (its gaps are hapax/unique/MI-d2, as for Indus, plus the length-by-type effect copied from the real lengths; the generator's 14-sign middle cap also shows). Merges: seq_raw M0 379 → M* 139; seq_strong 378 → 133; same ranking | **The frame grammar as a generator is incomplete in three specific ways, and all three are predictions of the credential model, not of a name-plus-title legend:** (1) texts are **copied whole within a city** (stock credentials, S344, S347); a lexicon of middles recombined freely with closers is refuted (80% vs 20% of repeated middles would change closer); (2) the **closer depends on the middle's end** (title phrases, S303), which sets text length and the end-of-text entropy; (3) the **object type sets the frame** (S29). With these, plus an open sign inventory (S309), the real corpus is indistinguishable from the synthetic ones at the battery's calibration floor on 33 of 50 statistics. **What remains unexplained is whole-text planning:** signs that attract or avoid each other across slots at a distance (S51, S67), the near-ban on using a sign twice (S88), a tighter length distribution and fewer double counts than any chain gives. No local mechanism tried here reproduces them; the next model needs a text-level constraint (a set of co-selected, non-repeating elements), i.e. a credential composed from a checklist rather than written left to right. Honest caveat: a plain order-2 chain beats the baseline slot grammar (247 vs 378), so the frame by itself is a poorer generator than local context; its value is in mechanisms (1)–(3), which a chain cannot express. Rests on MD + H (76% of texts); held-out sites agree; not merge-dependent. |

## Summary (updated after the push: 141 strategies)

### Scoreboard: strongest results of the push

| Finding | Rows | Strength | Replicated? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Statistical key-fitting as run here cannot read a planted or real known language: passing keys are wrong, and wrong keys beat the true key whenever only the common signs are decoded | S107, S113–S123 | Decisive | 8 objective, data and vocabulary variants |
| Identifiability holds only for a small (80-sign) syllabary. At 150+ signs, wrong keys win even with every sign decoded and the exact vocabulary known | S124–S127 | Strong (contrast controls) | 3 inventory sizes, 2 search lengths |
| Constructive: 40 outside anchors (of 150 signs) + right vocabulary → 86% of the remaining signs recovered | S129 | Strong (planted) | 2 anchor levels |
| Anchor route at Indus scale: 40–60 anchors for a 400-sign syllabary; about 20 (domain vocabulary) or 40 (generic dictionary) for a logo-syllabic script; the anchors also pick the language | S133–S137 | Strong (planted) | noisy anchors and wrong lexicon tested |
| The only published anchor set (Fuls 2024, 20 Linear Elamite values) gives no more Elamite words than chance, with a test shown to have power | S131, S132 | Solid null | 2 Elamite word lists |
| Functional dictionary of the 120 commonest signs (function, not sound); classes agree across Mohenjo-daro and Harappa 78% vs 21% chance | S139, S140 | Strong | cross-city |
| No candidate language (Tamil, Sanskrit, Sumerian, Akkadian, Telugu, Malayalam, Pali, Santali) stands out | S8, S8b, S31, S55 | Solid null | 2 corpora |
| Frame grammar (opener, marker, middle, closer, suffix) with non-local constraints beyond bigrams | S18, S36–S38, S50, S51, S67 | Strong | IM77 + Wells; out of sample (S44); earlier in-repo work agrees |
| Slot classes absent in planted syllabic text and Ur III legends | S37, S87, S89, S90 | Strong | Both transcriptions |
| Opener = seal-owning; suffix = tablets/sealings; mutually exclusive | S29, S29b, S66, S86 | Strong | Both transcriptions, within cities |
| Closers are a closed class (~200) that clusters by house and quarter | S40, S54, S62, S63, S63b, S85 | Moderate | Mohenjo-daro specific; Harappa confounded |
| Round (Gulf-type) seal tradition: no home frame; abroad, persons + numbers, "1 + person", own formulae (415-803-1) | S46, S46b, S74, S98b, S99–S104 | Strong (abroad), moderate (at home) | Robustness checks applied |
| Tablets as vouchers: count on one face, text on the other; texts keep fixed counts | S93, S93b, S94 | Moderate–strong | Incised-only control |
| Indus texts are formulae, not sentences (repetition 0.1–0.3 vs 0.7–0.8 for language) | S16, S88, S91b | Strong | Planted syllabic and logographic controls |
| Typology: suffixing, modifier before head (excludes West Asian head-initial languages) | S33, S35, S43 | Moderate (depends on reading direction) | — |

### Details

**No dictionary, and the key search could never have produced one.**
- Sumerian, Akkadian, Tamil and Sanskrit (on middles) all score on Indus inside the range they reach on other-language corpora; Telugu and Malayalam had no power (S8, S8b, S31, S55).
- **More fundamentally, the audit (S107, S113) shows that passing keys recover almost none of the true readings, even in planted controls and in real Ur III seal legends** (0–3 of 80). The window-hit objective prefers degenerate keys: the true planted key scores 1,313 hits vs 3,514 for the found key.
- So every earlier "PASS" or "power OK" detected language-derived text, not a correct key. Forcing a one-to-one key does not help: wrong injective keys still beat the true key, 2,339–2,483 vs 1,313 hits (S114). The objective is non-identifiable at this corpus size. More text does not help (S115). A whole-text coverage objective with a one-to-one key parses 100% of a planted Tamil corpus with 0 correct signs, better than the true key's 67% (S116, S117). **Short formulaic texts plus a large lexicon always admit a perfect-looking wrong reading.** Even with the 40 commonest signs anchored to their true values, the search recovers only 6 of the other 40 (S118), and a 15× larger corpus does not change this (S119). A syllable language model, the classical cipher-breaking objective, is also fooled (S120). So are clean whole-word texts (S122), and so is the exact true vocabulary (S123). The contrast case shows why: with a small syllabary of 80 signs the truth becomes the optimum (S124). With 150 or 300 signs it no longer is when only the top 80 are decoded (S125), it seemed to be again when *all* signs were decoded (S126), but a longer search finds wrong keys that beat the truth there too (S127). **What works:** with about 40 of 150 signs anchored from outside and the right vocabulary, the search recovers 86% of the rest (S129), and 56% even with a generic large dictionary. With 20 anchors it recovers only 20% (S130). A threshold of about 15–25% of the inventory is the target for any future decipherment. For a logo-syllabic script where the common words are word-signs (as the Indus frame suggests), about 20 anchors suffice with a domain vocabulary and about 40 with a generic dictionary (S135, S136). **The barrier is the long tail of rare signs in very short texts, plus an unknown language and vocabulary**, and a large wrong lexicon still fits perfectly (S117).
- Structure also argues against a phonetic key: repetition behaves like formulaic legends (S9, S16, S26, S88); doubled signs change function rather than spell syllables (S57, S58); foreign residues are too short for syllabic names (S53); sign variants do not track context (S22).

**What was found instead: a grammar, from data only** (GRAMMAR.md). Note: the opener / core / pre-ending / ending frame and the two stroke uses were already in `docs/INDUS-DECIPHERMENT-ATTEMPT.md`. This push re-derived them independently (from IM77) and added the controls and the new findings listed in GRAMMAR.md, "Relation to earlier work".

1. **A seal-text frame.** Opener (267-99 / 391-99 / 293-123-343) + unique middle + closer (X + jar/211/15/12/162/169) + optional suffix (176/1) (S18, S24, S36–S38).
   - Found without supervision (S37) and replicated on the independent Wells transcription (S30, S66, S67).
   - Holds out of sample on post-1977 sites (S44).
   - Its non-local constraints go beyond bigrams (S50, S51, S67).
2. **Name-like middles and title-like closers.**
   - Middles are unique and city-local (S17, S76) and combine freely with closers (S32); some are compounds (S34, S35).
   - Closers are a closed class (S40, S54), shared across cities (S25) yet clustered by house and quarter within Mohenjo-daro (S62, S63, S63b).
   - Quantities sit in the closing part (S68); each frame slot has its own human-figure sign (S77, S72).
3. **Two sub-systems in one grammar.** The opener goes with owned seals and the post-jar suffix with sealings, tablets and rods, within each city (S29, S29b, S66). They exclude each other inside a text (S51, S67).
4. **Two numeral usages.** Short and tall strokes pair with disjoint item classes (S70, S70b), but most are fixed number-compounds (S71); genuine counting shows mainly on trees (S23).
5. **The round-seal tradition.** Round (Gulf-type) and cylinder seals lack the home owner frame, even when found in Indus cities (S46; robust at known sites, S46b).
   - Found abroad, they carry person + number texts with the person sign first (S47, S74). Square Indus seals and pots abroad keep the home grammar (S74).
   - Numbered persons occur in 10 of 56 texts of this tradition vs 6 of 3,194 home texts (S98b); at home persons are never counted (S98).
   - It has its own formulae: 415-803-1-… at Ur and Dilmun, "1 + person" beside unique name-like strings (S99–S104). Its "1 + person" matches the Mesopotamian "1(diš) + name" way of listing individuals (S101).
   - Round seals found at home instead carry W140/W592 (S106), so the round format at home and abroad is not one homogeneous group.
   - The tradition favours the short-horned bull emblem (S65, S65b).
   - This is the best-defined population for a future outside anchor (ANCHORS §18).
5b. **Tablets as vouchers.** Two-sided Harappa tablets put a count on one face and a text on the other, almost never two counts (4 vs 34 expected, S94).
   - Texts keep a fixed count above chance, even on hand-incised tablets (S93, S93b).
   - The main multi-value voucher formula sits in one area of Mound F (S96).
   - The standard value may shift from 4 to 3 over time (S95, weakened by S95b).
6. **Typology.** Markers follow their hosts and modifiers precede heads (S33, S35, S36). This excludes head-initial West Asian languages as the frame's language, but it cannot separate Dravidian, Indo-Aryan, Munda or Burushaski (S43).
7. **Time.** The grammar is stable through Mohenjo-daro's sequence (S45). Tablet texts lengthen from Harappa 3A/B to 3C (S49), and the frame erodes at Dholavira stage 6 (S45).

**Controls added late in the push:**
- The paradigm finder finds no slot classes in planted syllabic text or Ur III legends, and only a few in Proto-Elamite (S87, S89, S90).
- Language-like text, syllabic or logographic, repeats signs at 0.7–0.8 of chance vs Indus 0.1–0.3 (S88).
- Long Indus texts are stacked formulae (S91, S91b).

**Corrections made during the push:**
- S9 was too strong; it is transcription-dependent (S16).
- S24's "prefixes" are middle-initial (S38).
- S70's "metrological systems" are mostly fixed compounds (S71).
- S63 at Harappa may be a phase effect (S63b).
- S17's "short texts spread" part did not replicate (S76).
- S46's person-sign enrichment at home was not robust (S46b); nor was S65's emblem at home (S65c).

**Frozen for new data:** R1–R13 in `prereg/preregistration-3-frozen.json`.
